Now the agent takes actions that shape what it observes, and AIXI is the ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Bayes-optimal policy"}]," over a universal mixture ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\xi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," of environments. It learns to act as well as if it knew the true environment ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],". Under the universal choice — ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cM","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," = all lower-semicomputable environments, prior ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"w_{\\nu} = 2^{-K(\\nu)}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," — the assumption \"",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu \\in \\cM","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"\" again becomes \"the universe is computable.\""]}],"\n",["$","h2",null,{"id":"prerequisites","children":"Prerequisites"}],"\n",["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"/agency/solomonoff-induction","children":"Solomonoff 23:["$","h2",null,{"id":"goal-and-roadmap","children":"Goal and Roadmap"}]
24:["$","p",null,{"children":"This exercise sheet builds towards three main results:"}]
25:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"On-policy value convergence"}]," (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#7-on-policy-value-convergence-of-bayes","children":"Section 7"}],"): the Bayesian mixture ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\xi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," learns to predict the value of any fixed policy as well as the true environment ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"AIXI can't be fooled"}]," (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#8-aixi-cannot-be-fooled-in-deterministic-environments","children":"Section 8"}],"): in deterministic environments, the Bayes-optimal agent is guaranteed non-zero value whenever optimal value is non-zero."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Self-optimizing property"}]," (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#11-proving-the-self-optimizing-property","children":"Section 11"}],", advanced stretch goal): the Bayes-optimal policy ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi_{\\xi}^{*}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," learns to ",["$","em",null,{"children":"act"}]," as well as if it knew ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", provided that any learnable policy can achieve this. This is the central theoretical justification for the AIXI agent."]}],"\n"]}]
26:["$","p",null,{"children":["A good target is to complete ",["$","a",null,{"href":"#7-on-policy-value-convergence-of-bayes","children":"Section 7"}]," and ",["$","a",null,{"href":"#8-aixi-cannot-be-fooled-in-deterministic-environments","children":"8"}],". The self-optimizing property (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#9-likelihood-ratios-are-martingales","children":"Sections 9–11"}],") requires substantial additional machinery (supermartingales, change of measure) and is an advanced stretch goal."]}]

2a:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"#0-properties-of-measures","children":"Sections 0–4"}]," establish the Bayesian RL framework: measure algebra, mixture properties, existence of optimal policies, dominance, and linearity."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"#5-the-expectimax-form-of-aixi","children":"Section 5"}]," derives the explicit expectimax form of AIXI."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"#6-bounding-expectation-differences-by-total-variation","children":"Sections 6–7"}]," prove ",["$","em",null,{"children":"on-policy value convergence"}],": ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"V_{\\xi}^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," and ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"V_{\\mu}^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," become indistinguishable for any fixed ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"#8-aixi-cannot-be-fooled-in-deterministic-environments","children":"Section 8"}]," shows ",["$","em",null,{"children":"AIXI can't be fooled"}],": the Bayes-optimal agent achieves non-zero value whenever optimal value is non-zero (in deterministic environments)."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"#9-likelihood-ratios-are-martingales","children":"Sections 9–11"}]," (advanced stretch goal) prove the ",["$","em",null,{"children":"self-optimizing property"}],": if any policy can learn to act optimally, ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi_{\\xi}^{*}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," inherits this."]}],"\n"]}]

2d:["$","section",null,{"id":"def-spaces","className":"my-4 rounded-md border border-indigo-300 bg-indigo-50 px-4 py-3","data-component":"definition","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Definition 0.1 (Spaces and notation)."}]," - ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cA","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": finite set of ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"actions"}]]}],["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cO","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": finite set of ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"observations"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cR \\subset [0,1]","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": finite set of ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"rewards"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cE := \\cO \\times \\cR","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": set of ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"percepts"}],"; ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"e_{t} = (o_{t}, r_{t}) \\equiv o_{t}r_{t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cH^{t} := (\\cA \\times \\cE)^{t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": set of all histories of length ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"t","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cH^{*} := \\cup_{t=0}^{\\infty} \\cH^{t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": set of all finite ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"histories"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cH^{\\infty} := (\\cA \\times \\cE)^{\\infty}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": set of all infinite histories"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\Delta S","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": set of all probability distributions over set ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"S","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\llbracket \\cdot \\rrbracket","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Iverson bracket"}],": ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\llbracket P \\rrbracket = 1","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," if ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"P","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," is true, ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"0","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," if false"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"t","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": current time step; ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"m","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": finite horizon; ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"1 \\leq t \\leq m","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"i, j, k","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": arbitrary integer indices"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"xy","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": concatenation"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\epsilon","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": the empty string/history"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\aes_{i:j}:= a_{i} e_{i}\\, a_{i+1}e_{i+1}\\,\\cdots\\, a_{j} e_{j}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": history segment from time ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"i","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," to ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"j","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\aes_{<t}:= a_{1} e_{1}\\, a_{2} e_{2} \\,\\cdots\\, a_{t-1}e_{t-1}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": history up to (but not including) time ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"t","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]]}],"\n"]}]]}]
2e:["$","section",null,{"id":"def-policy","className":"my-4 rounded-md border border-indigo-300 bg-indigo-50 px-4 py-3","data-component":"definition","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["Definition 0.2 (Policy ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],")."]}]," A ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"policy"}]," ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi : \\cH \\to \\Delta \\cA","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," maps each history to a probability distribution over actions. 76:["$","details",null,{"suppressHydrationWarning":true,"className":"my-3 rounded-md border border-zinc-200 px-3 py-2","children":[["$","summary",null,{"className":"cursor-pointer font-medium","children":"Solution"}],["$","div",null,{"className":"mt-2","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":["Write out the expectation difference using ",["$","a",null,{"href":"#def-expectation-p","children":"Definition 6.2"}],":"]}],["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\Ex_{P}[f] - \\Ex_{Q}[f] ~=~ \\sum_{\\omega \\in \\Omega}f(\\omega)\\big(P(\\omega) - Q(\\omega)\\big).","data-display":"1","className":"katex-display"}],["$","p",null,{"children":["Define ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"A^{+} := \\{\\omega \\in \\Omega : P(\\omega) \\geq Q(\\omega)\\}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," and ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"A^{-} := \\Omega \\setminus A^{+}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", and split the sum:"]}],["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\Ex_{P}[f] - \\Ex_{Q}[f] ~=~ \\sum_{\\omega \\in A^+}f(\\omega)\\big(P(\\omega) - Q(\\omega)\\big) ~+~ \\sum_{\\omega \\in A^-}f(\\omega)\\big(P(\\omega) - Q(\\omega)\\big).","data-display":"1","className":"katex-display"}],["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","em",null,{"children":["Bounding the sum over ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"A^{+}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"."]}]," On ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"A^{+}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"P(\\omega) - Q(\\omega) \\geq 0","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," and ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"f(\\omega) \\leq c","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", so:"]}],["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\begin{aligned}\\sum_{\\omega \\in A^+}f(\\omega)\\big(P(\\omega) - Q(\\omega)\\big) ~&\\leq~ c \\sum_{\\omega \\in A^+}\\big(P(\\omega) - Q(\\omega)\\big) \\\\ ~&=~ c \\cdot \\big(P(A^{+}) - Q(A^{+})\\big) \\\\ ~&\\leq~ c \\cdot \\sup_{S} |P(S) - Q(S)| \\\\ ~&=~ c \\cdot \\TV[\\Omega](P, Q).\\end{aligned}","data-display":"1","className":"katex-display"}],["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","em",null,{"children":["Bounding the sum over ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"A^{-}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"."]}]," On ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"A^{-}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"P(\\omega) - \\Ex_{Q}[f] \\leq c \\cdot \\TV[\\Omega](P,Q) + 0 = c \\cdot \\TV[\\Omega](P,Q)","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"."]}],["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","em",null,{"children":"The other direction."}]," Swapping ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"P","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," and ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"Q","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": define ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\tilde{A}^{+} = \\{\\omega : Q(\\omega) \\geq P(\\omega)\\} = A^{-}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],". 7f:["$","p",null,{"children":["The Bayesian agent uses the mixture ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\xi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," because the true environment ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," is unknown. A natural question: does planning with ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\xi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," eventually become as good as planning with ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"? The following theorem says ",["$","em",null,{"children":"yes"}],": the value of any fixed policy ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", as evaluated by ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\xi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", converges to the value under the true environment ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", along histories that ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," actually generates. (If the true environment is ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," then we don't care about the behaviour of the Bayesian agent on histories that have ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-probability zero: such histories will never be observed anyway.) This tells us that the Bayesian mixture \"learns\" to predict the true environment's value, on policy."]}]

97:["$","section",null,{"id":"def-selfopt","className":"my-4 rounded-md border border-indigo-300 bg-indigo-50 px-4 py-3","data-component":"definition","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Definition 9.1 (Self-optimizing)."}]," Fix a historic policy ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],". 98:["$","section",null,{"id":"thm-selfopt","className":"my-5 border-l-4 border-violet-500 bg-violet-50 px-4 py-3 rounded-r","data-component":"theorem","children":["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["Theorem 9.2 (Self-optimizing; (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-hutter-24uaibook2","children":"Hutter et al. 2024"}],", Theorem 7.5.2))."]}]," Let ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\cM","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," be finite and fix a historic policy ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\pi","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],". 99:["$","section",null,{"id":"def-supermartingale","className":"my-4 rounded-md border border-indigo-300 bg-indigo-50 px-4 py-3","data-component":"definition","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Definition 9.3 (Supermartingale)."}]," A sequence of functions ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{t} : \\cH^{t}\\to \\mathbb{R}_{\\geq 0}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," (each depending on the history ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\aes_{1:t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],") is a ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-",["$","strong",null,{"children":"supermartingale"}]," if ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\Ex_{\\mu^\\pi}[X_{t} \\mid \\aes_{<t}] \\leq X_{t-1}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," for all ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"t","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"; that is,"]}],["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\sum_{\\aes_t}\\pi(a_{t} \\mid \\aes_{<t})\\, \\mu(e_{t} \\mid \\aes_{<t}a_{t})\\, X_{t}(\\aes_{1:t}) ~\\leq~ X_{t-1}(\\aes_{<t}).","data-display":"1","className":"katex-display"}],["$","p",null,{"children":["If equality holds, it is a ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-",["$","strong",null,{"children":"martingale"}],"."]}]]}]
9a:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Intuition."}]," A supermartingale is a quantity whose expected future value, given the present, is no larger than its current value: it cannot \"drift upward on average.\" A martingale is the equality case: expected future value equals current value. The key example is the ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"likelihood ratio"}]," ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}(\\aes_{1:t}) := \\nu(\\aes_{1:t})/\\mu(\\aes_{1:t})","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],": how much more (or less) likely the observed history is under ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\nu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," than under the true environment ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],". Under ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", this ratio is a martingale: the true environment does not, on average, favour any alternative ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\nu","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," over itself. Non-negativity plus the (super)martingale structure forces ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," to converge (by Doob's theorem below), which is the engine behind the self-optimizing proof: it lets us separate environments that remain plausible (",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,\\infty}> 0","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],") from those that are eventually ruled out (",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,\\infty}= 0","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"), and handle each case differently in ",["$","a",null,{"href":"#11-proving-the-self-optimizing-property","children":"Section 11"}],"."]}]
9b:["$","div",null,{"id":"fact-doob","children":["$","div",null,{"id":"$undefined","className":"my-4 rounded-md border-l-4 px-4 py-3 border-sky-500 bg-sky-50","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Fact 9.4 (Doob's supermartingale convergence)."}]," If ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"(X_{t})_{t \\geq 0}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," is a non-negative ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-supermartingale, then there exists a function ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\infty} : \\cH^{\\infty} \\to \\mathbb{R}_{\\geq 0}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," of the infinite history such that ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{t}(\\aes_{1:t}) \\to X_{\\infty}(\\aes_{1:\\infty})","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," as ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"t \\to \\infty","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", with ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\infty}(\\aes_{1:\\infty}) < \\infty","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", for ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-almost every infinite history ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\aes_{1:\\infty}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],". That is:"]}],["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}\\!\\Big(\\Big\\{\\aes_{1:\\infty}\\in (\\cA \\times \\cE)^{\\infty} : X_{t}(\\aes_{1:t}) \\not\\to X_{\\infty}(\\aes_{1:\\infty})\\Big\\}\\Big) ~=~ 0.","data-display":"1","className":"katex-display"}]]}]}]
9c:["$","p",null,{"children":["For each ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\nu \\in \\cM","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", define the likelihood ratio"]}]
9d:["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}: \\cH^{t} \\to \\mathbb{R}_{\\geq 0}, \\qquad X_{\\nu,t}(\\aes_{1:t}) ~:=~ \\frac{\\nu(\\aes_{1:t})}{\\mu(\\aes_{1:t})}, \\qquad X_{\\nu,0}:= 1.","data-display":"1","className":"katex-display"}]
9e:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}]," is a ",["$","em",null,{"children":"function"}]," of the history ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\aes_{1:t}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],", not a number: different histories give different values. Throughout this section and the next, any unqualified statement of the form \"",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}\\geq c","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"\" or \"",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}\\to X_{\\nu,\\infty}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"\" is shorthand for \"",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}(\\aes_{1:t}) \\geq c","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"\" or \"",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"X_{\\nu,t}(\\aes_{1:t}) \\to X_{\\nu,\\infty}(\\aes_{1:\\infty})","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"\" holding ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-almost surely, i.e. on every infinite history outside a set of ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\mu^{\\pi}","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],"-measure zero. a0:["$","details",null,{"suppressHydrationWarning":true,"className":"my-3 rounded-md border border-zinc-200 px-3 py-2","children":[["$","summary",null,{"className":"cursor-pointer font-medium","children":"Solution"}],["$","div",null,{"className":"mt-2","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":["We need to compute ",["$","span",null,{"data-tex":"\\Ex_{\\mu^\\pi}[X_{\\nu,t}\\mid \\aes_{<t}]","data-display":"$undefined","className":"$undefined"}],".
